------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Agent Lang states that the president went up to the roof with Judge Gavèlle and that Judge Gavèlle returned alone via the elevator. There is no sign of the president getting on the elevator at all, implying that the president did not leave the roof via the elevator. 

However, Evidence 6 mentions that the concrete was broken and the earth exposed after Excelsius Winner dug up President Wang's corpse. This suggests that the president's body was moved from its original location, possibly indicating that the president was initially somewhere else and then brought to the roof.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial reasoning rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

None of the spatial reasoning rules directly apply to the contradiction identified here. The rules provided deal with falling objects and relative positions within enclosed spaces, which do not directly relate to the movement of the president's body or the presence of Judge Gavèlle on the roof.

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Since no applicable rules exist from the provided list, we skip this step.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial movement implied by the testimonies and the evidence. According to Lang's testimonies, the president was on the roof with Judge Gavèlle and did not leave via the elevator. However, Evidence 6 suggests that the president's body was moved to the roof, indicating that the president was originally elsewhere and then brought to the roof, which contradicts Lang's claim that the president never left the roof.

This suggests that Phoenix might have been involved in placing the Coldkiller bottle in the victim's hand, which contradicts his testimony of simply shoving the victim and leaving the scene.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Iris's testimonies describe a fight scene involving Maya and Elise, where Elise's back was to a stone lantern. However, Evidence 20 states that the Stone Lantern was not lit the night of the crime. This suggests that the visibility conditions were poor, which could affect whether such a detailed observation could be made.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

The rule "If XXX blocks the view of YYY then YYY cannot be seen" does not directly apply here because there is no indication that the lantern itself blocked anyone's view. However, the rule "If XXX is in the same room as YYY then XXX can potentially see YYY" could be considered indirectly related, as it implies potential visibility based on location. Here, we need to infer that without light, visibility would be significantly reduced, making it difficult to observe the specific positioning of characters.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

While no direct rule applies, we can infer from the rule "If XXX is in the same room as YYY then XXX can potentially see YYY" that visibility is a key factor in determining whether someone can see another person. Since the stone lantern was not lit, it would be challenging to make detailed observations about the positioning of individuals, especially noting that Elise's back was to the lantern.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial reasoning used here involves understanding that the lack of lighting from the stone lantern would create a dark environment, making it unlikely that Iris could have observed the precise positioning of Elise and Maya, particularly noting that Elise's back was to the lantern. This contradicts Iris's detailed account of the fight scene.

However, Evidence 12 (Jinxie's Statement) mentions that she saw the demon fleeing toward the foyer. Testimony 4 also indicates that L'Belle fled down the hallway to his right, which would logically lead him away from the foyer if he were in the corridor leading to the Fox Chamber.

We can apply the following rule: If XXX is inside YYY and YYY is to the right of ZZZ then XXX is to the right of the ZZZ.

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

In this case, we can infer that the hallway leading to the Fox Chamber is to the east of the foyer based on the Crime Scene Diagram (Evidence 5). If L'Belle was in the hallway to the east and fled to his right, he would be moving further away from the foyer, not towards it. This contradicts Jinxie's statement about seeing the demon flee toward the foyer.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

None of the listed rules directly apply to the contradiction identified, but the spatial reasoning involves understanding the layout of the manor as described in the Crime Scene Diagram and interpreting L'Belle's direction of movement relative to the location of the foyer.

According to Testimony 1, O'Conner saw Juniper dragging a large mat from behind the stage. However, Evidence 18, the Stage Set Up Photos, shows a tall construction screen between the stage and the vantage point near the maintenance area. If there was a construction screen blocking the view, it would be impossible for O'Conner to see Juniper dragging the mat from behind the stage.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial reasoning rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

The spatial reasoning rule that applies here is: "If XXX is locked then XXX cannot be passed through". Although this rule is about locking, we can infer a similar principle that if something is blocked or obstructed, it cannot be seen through.

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

In this context, the construction screen acts similarly to a lock, preventing visibility through it. Since O'Conner claims he could see Juniper dragging the mat, but the presence of the construction screen contradicts this possibility, we can conclude that his testimony is inconsistent with the evidence.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

None of the listed rules directly apply, but the spatial reasoning used here is based on obstruction. The construction screen obstructs the view from the vantage point near the maintenance area to behind the stage, making it impossible for O'Conner to observe Juniper's actions as he claimed.

**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, we know that Melissa Foster took a photo of the fight on Dusky Bridge from the south side. According to Testimony 6, the victim (Valerie Hawthorne) was stabbed in the back and fell approximately 10 yards after turning around and trying to run away. Evidence 2 provides a map of Dusky Bridge, indicating that the bridge is broken midway towards the east and connects to the parking lot towards the west. Additionally, it states that the view from the south of the bridge to the parking lot is obstructed by huge rocks.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

The spatial reasoning rule that applies here is: If XXX is hit from the front then XXX falls backward. However, since Valerie was stabbed in the back, we can infer that she would fall forward, not backward.

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

The rule "If XXX is hit from behind then XXX falls forward" applies here. Since Valerie was stabbed in the back, she would likely fall forward, towards the broken part of the bridge or into the river below. This contradicts the fact that Melissa could see the fight from the south side of the bridge, as the view to the parking lot (west) is obstructed by huge rocks, implying that the broken part of the bridge (east) is where the action occurred.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Given that the view from the south to the west is obstructed by huge rocks, Melissa would not be able to see the victim running towards the parking lot (west). Instead, the victim would likely fall towards the broken part of the bridge (east), which would be visible from the south side. Therefore, the description of the victim running and being stabbed in the back, falling 10 yards, contradicts the physical layout of the bridge as described in the map.

Evidence 14 mentions that a winning lottery ticket for half a million dollars was found during a body search of Maggey. This suggests that Maggey might have been involved with the victim's money or possessions, but it does not directly relate to the spatial arrangement of objects.

However, Evidence 12 provides a photo of the crime scene showing two dining tables separated by a folding screen. The victim collapsed on the table, and there is a coffee cup knocked over with a coffee stain on the tablecloth. According to Testimony 4, "the little fidget picked up the cup with his free hand and took a sip." This implies that the victim was holding the cup and drinking from it when he collapsed.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial reasoning rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

The rule "If XXX is shot from YYY direction then the bullet comes from YYY direction" does not apply since the victim died of poisoning, not a gunshot. **Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

None of the listed rules apply directly to the spatial contradiction identified.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial contradiction arises from the fact that the coffee cup was knocked over and the tablecloth was stained by coffee, suggesting that the victim did not simply collapse while holding the cup. If the victim was holding the cup and took a sip, as stated in Testimony 4, it would be unlikely for the cup to be knocked over and spill coffee without the victim dropping it or someone else interfering. However, the testimonies do not mention anyone else interfering with the cup, and the victim's collapse is described as a result of taking a sip, which contradicts the state of the spilled coffee.

- **Evidence 2 (Space Center Diagram):** This evidence provides a layout of the Space Center, including the connection between the main building and Launch Pad 1 via a passage between the mid-level of Launch Pad 1 and the 3rd-floor lounge of the main building.
  
- **Testimony 1 (Starbuck):** "Like always, I took the elevator down to the middle level and headed for the corridor."

None of the spatial reasoning rules directly apply to the movement described in the testimony. However, we can use the provided diagram to infer the spatial arrangement and identify potential contradictions.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the description of the Space Center Diagram (Evidence 2) and the testimony given by Starbuck (Testimony 1). According to the diagram, the passage connecting the main building and Launch Pad 1 is located between the mid-level of Launch Pad 1 and the 3rd-floor lounge of the main building. If Starbuck took the elevator down to the middle level and then headed for the corridor, he would have had to pass through the boarding lounge to reach the passage. However, the autopsy report (Evidence 1) states that the victim was discovered in the third-floor boarding lounge, suggesting that the victim was already there when the attack occurred. 

Given that the victim was found in the boarding lounge and the testimony suggests that Starbuck took the elevator down to the middle level and then moved towards the corridor, it implies that Starbuck would have had to pass through the boarding lounge to reach the passage. This contradicts the idea that the victim was already in the boarding lounge when the attack happened, as Starbuck's movement would have required him to be present in the same area.

However, Evidence 3, the Study Photo, shows the victim lying next to floor-to-ceiling windows with clear glass, covered by books. If Dr. Buff was struck from behind, he would likely fall forward or sideways, not necessarily towards the window where he is covered by books.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial reasoning rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

The spatial reasoning rule that applies here is: 
"If XXX is hit from the front then XXX falls backward"

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

The rule "If XXX is hit from the front then XXX falls backward" suggests that if Dr. Buff was hit from the front, he would fall backward. However, Atishon's testimony indicates that Dr. Buff was hit from behind, which would typically cause him to fall forward or sideways, not backward towards the window.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, the rule does apply, and it helps us understand that if Dr. Buff was hit from behind, he would not naturally fall towards the window where he is covered by books. This contradicts the spatial positioning described in the Study Photo.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

Shih-na's testimonies suggest that she was in the Babahl side of the embassy during both fires and was searching for the Yatagarasu. Specifically, she mentions hearing a scream from the next room over and being unable to catch a glimpse of the Yatagarasu despite being in the adjacent room. 

However, Evidence 29 states that the Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall and into the next room over. If Shih-na was indeed in the Babahl side of the embassy and the Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall into the next room, it implies that the Yatagarasu could have been seen by Shih-na if she was close enough to the wall or if the wall was open.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial reasoning rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

The spatial reasoning rule that applies here is:
- If XXX surrounds YYY then YYY is enclosed by XXX

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

The rule "If XXX surrounds YYY then YYY is enclosed by XXX" does not directly apply to the contradiction identified. However, the concept of adjacency and movement through walls (specifically the revolving fireplace wall) is relevant to understanding the spatial dynamics involved.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial arrangement and movement within the embassy. Shih-na's testimonies indicate she was in the Babahl side of the embassy and close enough to hear a scream from the next room over, suggesting proximity to the dividing wall. Yet, the fact that the Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall into the next room implies that Shih-na should have had a chance to see the Yatagarasu if the wall was open or if she was near it. Her inability to catch a glimpse of the Yatagarasu contradicts the spatial possibility of seeing someone moving through an adjacent wall.
